- **citation-audit**: Systematic bibliometric audit of citing-paper author affiliations across OpenAlex and Dimensions Analytics. - **citation-audit**: Audit existing citations in a manuscript for claim-support alignment, primary-vs-review mismatch, causal overreach, and unsupported assertions, and check that each reference's identifier resolves to the work the reference describes. - **citation-audit**: Zero-context verification that every cited bibliography entry is real, correctly attributed, and actually supports the sentence citing it - catching hallucinated references, wrong metadata, and wrong-context citations with per-entry KEEP/FIX/REPLACE/REMOVE verdicts. Use before any submission (paper or thesis) or when the user asks to check citations, verify references, or audit the bibliography. ([wookat/ai-research-skills](https://github.com/wookat/ai-research-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/citation-audit) · ★6)
- **citation-audit**: Three-layer check on every bib entry: the work **exists** (with rank-1 sanity check), the **metadata** matches canonical sources, the **context** in our sentence is something the cited paper actually establishes. Wrong-context is the failure class that survives naïve audits; Crossref rank-1 trust is the failure class that produces fabrications at scale. ([Minions-Land/Science-Discovery](https://github.com/Minions-Land/Science-Discovery/tree/HEAD/Ours/FigureDraw3/arms/minionsos-v3/skills/citation-audit) · ★5 · 5 copies)

- **cite-resolve**: Verify references against bibliographic registries (Crossref, DataCite, arXiv, Semantic Scholar) instead of writing DOIs, venues, volumes, pages or author lists from memory. Use whenever you are about to cite a paper, build a bibliography or BibTeX file, turn web-search results (Exa, web_search, etc., which return title and URL but no DOI) into references, or check a reference list someone gave you. Triggers: "cite", "reference list", "bibliography", "BibTeX", "DOI", "check these references", 「引用して」「参考文献」「文献リスト」「書誌を確かめて」「DOIを付けて」「bibを作って」. ([TMLlaboratory/cite-resolve](https://github.com/TMLlaboratory/cite-resolve/tree/HEAD/skills/cite-resolve))
- **cite-review**: Use when citations in a LaTeX manuscript need checking against what the cited papers actually say — prose that may overstate, understate, or mischaracterise a source. Not for writing new prose or creating citations; that is literature-writing. ([yqi96/warranted](https://github.com/yqi96/warranted/tree/HEAD/skills/cite-review) · ★3)
- **cite-seq-adt-count-processing**: Use when raw ADT/CITE-seq FASTQ files (I1/R1/R2) from hashed samples need to be converted into antibody-by-cell UMI count matrices, optionally after removing index-hopped reads from combined NovaSeq GEX+ADT data. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cite-seq-adt-count-processing))
- **cite-seq-adt-normalization**: Use when raw ADT/protein UMI counts from droplet-based single-cell assays (CITE-seq, ASAP-seq, TEA-seq) must be normalized and denoised before clustering, or when comparing dsb against CLR normalization for protein-based analyses. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cite-seq-adt-normalization))
- **cite-seq-adt-relative-abundance-transformation**: Use when preparing paired transcriptome–surface protein (CITE-seq or REAP-seq) data as training targets for cTP-net or comparable protein-imputation models: raw ADT barcode counts must be converted to per-cell relative abundances before model training. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cite-seq-adt-relative-abundance-transformation))
- **cite-seq-atac-seq-multimodal-integration**: Use when clustering cells measured across multiple modalities (e.g. CITE-seq RNA+ADT, or SHARE-seq/SNARE-seq RNA+ATAC) and you need integrated unsupervised embeddings comparable to totalVI/MultiVI/MOFA+ methods. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cite-seq-atac-seq-multimodal-integration))
- **cite-seq-batch-correction-comparison**: Use when combining two or more CITE-seq datasets (e.g., pbmc_1k_protein_v3 and pbmc_10k_protein_v3) and you need to decide whether Seurat batch correction changes the cell groups identified by multimodal LinQ-View clustering. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cite-seq-batch-correction-comparison))
- **cite-seq-cell-embedding-clustering-subsetting**: Use when scME joint embeddings from CITE-seq BMNC data must be subdivided into cell-type subclusters and the subdivision needs validation from differential protein (CDT ADT) markers like CD69 and CD25 rather than genes. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cite-seq-cell-embedding-clustering-subsetting))
- **cite-seq-count-matrix-processing**: Use when raw ADT/protein UMI count matrices from a CITE-seq experiment are available (e.g., raw and filtered Cell Ranger outputs) and you need to characterize ambient antibody background before normalization, or verify that empty-droplet background reflects protein levels in cells. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cite-seq-count-matrix-processing))
- **cite-seq-dual-modality-model-implementation**: Use when you have paired CITE-seq RNA gene counts and raw protein ADT counts (CSV, rows=cells) and need a joint cell embedding that retains modality-specific complementary information, not just shared structure. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cite-seq-dual-modality-model-implementation))
- **cite-seq-expression-normalization**: Use when raw CITE-seq or scRNA-seq count matrices (e.g., GEO GSE100866 CBMC data) must be normalized with log2(1+X) before marker selection or k-NN label classification. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cite-seq-expression-normalization))
- **cite-seq-gene-protein-association-analysis**: Use when analyzing CITE-seq or other single-cell multiomic AnnData with raw gene counts in X and protein responses in obsm, to find conditionally dependent gene–protein pairs with calibrated FDR control rather than marginal correlations. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics/tree/HEAD/collections/multi-omics/v1/leaves/cite-seq-gene-protein-association-analysis))
- **cite-seq-hashtag-count-normalization**: Use when cell-hashing CITE-seq data must be demultiplexed: a hashtag count matrix per cell barcode exists and each cell needs a sample identity call plus doublet/negative classification before downstream GEX/ADT analysis. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cite-seq-hashtag-count-normalization))
- **cite-seq-hashtag-umi-counting**: Use when hashed CITE-seq ADT FASTQs (I1/R1/R2 per sample) must be converted into per-cell hashtag UMI count matrices prior to demultiplexing sample identities in a gExcite/Snakemake workflow. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cite-seq-hashtag-umi-counting))
- **cite-seq-multimodal-clustering**: Use when analyzing CITE-seq data with both transcriptome and surface-protein (ADT) modalities and the goal is joint cell-population clustering plus quantitative purity scoring in both modalities. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cite-seq-multimodal-clustering))
- **cite-seq-multimodal-data-preprocessing**: Use when raw CITE-seq data (paired scRNA-seq and ADT counts from platforms like 10X Chromium) must be filtered, normalized, and packaged for joint multimodal clustering benchmarking. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cite-seq-multimodal-data-preprocessing))
- **cite-seq-multimodal-distance-fusion**: Use when analyzing CITE-seq data with a limited number of ADT features (e.g. &lt;50) where unimodal or naive joint analysis fails to separate subsets (e.g. memory vs naive CD4+ T cells), or when distinguishing antigen-specific B cell populations ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cite-seq-multimodal-distance-fusion))
- **cite-seq-multimodal-embedding-generation**: Use when CITE-seq or other multimodal single-cell data (same cells measured across modalities, e.g. RNA + ADT matrices) needs integrated embeddings to cluster cells into major and minor cell types. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cite-seq-multimodal-embedding-generation))
- **cite-seq-multimodal-integration**: Use when evaluating or reproducing multimodal CITE-seq integration, specifically testing whether ALRA imputation of RNA counts improves agreement between RNA-space and ADT protein-space pairwise cell distances. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cite-seq-multimodal-integration))
- **cite-seq-multimodal-integration-benchmarking**: Use when comparing integration algorithms (e.g. scMerge2, scMerge2-h, Seurat, Harmony, scVI, Scanorama) on a multi-batch CITE-seq or scRNA-seq dataset where both batch labels and cell-type annotations are available for evaluation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cite-seq-multimodal-integration-benchmarking))
- **cite-seq-multimodal-reference-building**: Use when a CITE-seq reference with paired mRNA and surface protein measurements is available but query samples are mRNA-only, and you need to infer query surface protein levels from reference mapping. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cite-seq-multimodal-reference-building))
- **cite-seq-protein-based-cell-type-annotation**: Use when a CITE-seq dataset contains cell-surface protein abundance (e.g., PBMC antibody-derived tags) and trusted cell-type labels are needed as reference ground truth for benchmarking clustering or label-validation methods. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cite-seq-protein-based-cell-type-annotation))
- **cite-seq-protein-differential-activity-analysis**: Use when analyzing a multimodal CITE-seq/PBMC h5ad atlas (&gt;200k cells, 228 proteins) to rank surface proteins by differential activity in PCA space without clustering, and to compare that ranking against cluster-based DE baselines. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cite-seq-protein-differential-activity-analysis))
- **cite-seq-protein-distance-constraint-generation**: Use when a CITE-seq dataset provides protein (ADT) measurements on the same cells as mRNA counts, and the goal is to improve mRNA clustering accuracy or enforce biologically consistent cluster labels (e.g., CD4/CD8 T cell subtypes) via pairwise constraints. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cite-seq-protein-distance-constraint-generation))
- **cite-seq-protein-gene-integration**: Use when paired CITE-seq or spatial-CITE-seq measurements (mRNA counts plus ADT protein counts) must be fused into a single latent space for joint clustering or spatial domain detection, e.g. GSE213264 human tonsil (2491 spots, 28,417 genes, 283 proteins). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics/tree/HEAD/collections/multi-omics/v1/leaves/cite-seq-protein-gene-integration))
- **cite-seq-protein-imputation-from-scrna**: Use when you have a UMI-based scRNA-seq count matrix and want per-cell relative abundance estimates of surface protein immunophenotype markers, or when benchmarking imputation against raw/denoised RNA baselines or Seurat v3 anchor transfer. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cite-seq-protein-imputation-from-scrna))
- **cite-seq-protein-mrna-correlation-analysis**: Use when evaluating whether a denoising/imputation method improves scRNA-seq data using CITE-seq ADT protein abundances as ground truth, e.g. reproducing Fig. 6 protein-mRNA co-expression and CD3E detection-rate analyses. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cite-seq-protein-mrna-correlation-analysis))
- **cite-seq-protein-normalisation-dsb**: Use when raw 'Antibody capture' counts from a 10x CITE-seq run must be background-corrected before annotation or multimodal integration, and both the filtered and raw Cell Ranger matrices are available. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics/tree/HEAD/collections/multi-omics/v1/leaves/cite-seq-protein-normalisation-dsb))
- **cite-seq-protein-rna-integration**: Use when CITE-seq data pairs surface-protein abundance (e.g. CD16/CD56 ADTs) with RNA counts for the same genes and the goal is to test whether RNA denoising recovers protein-defined sub-population structure. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cite-seq-protein-rna-integration))
- **cite-seq-raw-count-preprocessing**: Use when a holdout CITE-seq UMI count matrix must be quality-filtered and LogNormalized without SAVER-X denoising to run the denoising-ablation of cTP-net and measure the correlation drop versus the denoised baseline. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cite-seq-raw-count-preprocessing))
- **cite-seq-surface-protein-expression-summarization**: Use when you have scMM output from CITE-seq (transcriptome & surface protein) data and need cluster-level multimodal feature heatmaps to interpret a high-resolution latent-space clustering, e.g. reproducing the PBMC ~54-cluster figure. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cite-seq-surface-protein-expression-summarization))
- **cite-source**: Register a published source for an equation, correlation or constant used in a model, with where it is in the source, its valid range, and a check that it was transcribed correctly. Use the moment one appears. ([faveric/faveric-modelica-skills](https://github.com/faveric/faveric-modelica-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/modelling/cite-source) · ★6)
- **cite-sources**: Generate properly formatted citations and references from research ([David-Li0406/meta-skill-evloving](https://github.com/David-Li0406/meta-skill-evloving/tree/HEAD/skill-flow/data/skills-refined-36k/skillsmp/cite-sources) · ★5)
- **cite-sources**: Track, validate, and cite external sources with working URLs ([synapseradio/ai-skills](https://github.com/synapseradio/ai-skills/tree/HEAD/extensions/thinkies/skills/cite-sources) · ★2 · 3 copies)
- **cite-sources**: Format research findings with a source for every claim. Use when reporting research results. ([brefledev/roam-sweet-roam](https://github.com/brefledev/roam-sweet-roam/tree/HEAD/examples/bob/skills/cite-sources) · ★1)
- **cite-sources**: Format references and check that references are real with the Arabic Scholar tools. Use when the user asks to cite a paper, build a reference list or bibliography, convert between APA, MLA, Chicago, Harvard and BibTeX, or verify references, or says "وثّق", "توثيق المراجع", "قائمة المراجع", "تحقق من المرجع". ([EngDawood/arabic-scholar-mcp-server](https://github.com/EngDawood/arabic-scholar-mcp-server/tree/HEAD/skills/cite-sources) · ★1)
- **cite-sources**: Use when writing or checking citations in findings or briefs; defines the house citation format and what counts as a source. ([Geenie-AI/skills](https://github.com/Geenie-AI/skills/tree/HEAD/tests/agentic-architect/scenarios/S09-skill-assignment/fixtures/repo/dot-claude/skills/cite-sources))
- **cite-sources**: 学術文献・論文を主張の根拠にする、または参考文献リストを作るときに、査読とDOIを確認する。 ([honahuku/dotfiles](https://github.com/honahuku/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/cite-sources))
- **cite-sources**: 등록된 문서를 검색해 찾은 근거로만 답하고 출처를 다는 방법. 문서에 관한 질문에 답하는 에이전트가 쓴다. ([njh6803/agent-os](https://github.com/njh6803/agent-os/tree/HEAD/plugins/skills/cite-sources))
- **cite-sources**: Collect facts from the web with one URL and the date read per fact; a fact without a source is not reported. ([yolol312/goodboy](https://github.com/yolol312/goodboy/tree/HEAD/skills/cite-sources))
- **cite-specific-section-numbers**: Anchor document analysis to exact sections or pages so readers can verify the supporting passage. ([alivirgo/Major-AI-Skills](https://github.com/alivirgo/Major-AI-Skills/tree/HEAD/skills/cite-specific-section-numbers) · ★3)
- **cite-style-converter**: Convert academic citations between APA, MLA, IEEE, and Harvard styles with batch processing and format validation. Trigger when users ask to convert, check, or fix their references, mention specific citation styles, or talk about their bibliography. ([serejaris/kimi-skills](https://github.com/serejaris/kimi-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/cite-style-converter) · ★13 · 5 copies)
- **cite-sync**: Regenerate each project's docs/refs/manifest.toml from the workspace citation registry. Walks docs/ and paper/ for [ns:key] tokens with external namespaces, looks each up in research/refs/registry.toml, and writes the public-facing subset (title + description, no internal paths) to the project manifest. Use when the user invokes /cite-sync, /cite-sync &lt;slug&gt;, or asks to 'sync the manifest', 'rebuild the refs manifest', 'regenerate citations manifest'. ([hsigstad/research-kit](https://github.com/hsigstad/research-kit/tree/HEAD/skills/cite-sync))
- **cite-them-all**: Academic reference agent that identifies claims needing citations, searches PubMed/bioRxiv/medRxiv via MCP tools, and adds properly formatted references to Markdown manuscripts ([erafat/skills](https://github.com/erafat/skills/tree/HEAD/cite-them-all) · ★17)
- **cite-verify**: Verify academic citations, bibliography metadata, DOI/arXiv/OpenAlex/Semantic Scholar consistency, claim-reference alignment, retraction risk, and whether cited sources actually support manuscript claims. Use for Cite Verify, citation audit, reference checking, DOI validation, claim support, broken citations, or 引用核验. ([JasperPWang/lab-codex-skills](https://github.com/JasperPWang/lab-codex-skills/tree/HEAD/cite-verify) · ★1)
- **cite-verify**: Verify a LaTeX paper's citations: every \cite key exists in the .bib, and every .bib entry is a real paper whose title, year and authors match Crossref or arXiv. Flags fabricated, mismatched, missing and unused references, and never invents one. Use whenever you add, edit or review citations or a .bib file, before submission, or when the user says check references, verify citations, or /cite-verify. ([adithyanraj03/dsh-skills-pack](https://github.com/adithyanraj03/dsh-skills-pack/tree/HEAD/skills/cite-verify))
- **cite-verify**: Verify citation integrity, detect AI-hallucinated references, validate DOIs against official CrossRef/arXiv APIs, and locate authentic original literature sources. ([Marvin-LiJunZan/skills-mcp](https://github.com/Marvin-LiJunZan/skills-mcp/tree/HEAD/skills/cite-verify))
- **cite-verify**: Use this skill whenever a response would include: a paper citation, arxiv ID, DOI, author name, publication venue, year of publication, a URL to a paper or repo, a claim about what a specific paper proves or proposes, or a statement like "X was introduced in [paper]" or "according to [author]". Also trigger when the user asks to find references for a topic, verify a citation they provide, or when building a related-work section. Training data citations are unreliable -- authors, titles, venues, and URLs are all hallucination-prone. Never trust memory for citation facts. Always verify before s… ([moonshineTP/ATTT-20252](https://github.com/moonshineTP/ATTT-20252/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/cite-verify) · 2 copies)
- **cite-verify**: Verify references (any discipline) at two levels. (1) Metadata — compare each entry's authors, title, year, journal, volume, pages, and DOI against Crossref, DataCite, arXiv, and Europe PMC (PubMed) records, and flag fabricated or nonexistent entries, preprints that have since been formally published, retractions and errata, and missing or unused citation keys. (2) Claim support — for sentences in the text such as "So-and-so proved X [n]", check whether the cited work actually says so. Use when checking a .bib file, an RIS export (Zotero / EndNote), a reference list in any citation style at t… ([OIerYangJZ/Science-Skill](https://github.com/OIerYangJZ/Science-Skill/tree/HEAD/skills/cite-verify))
- **citeanything**: Create verifiable evidence citations for data claims. Use after obtaining data from web APIs or KB documents to create citations with source links, text anchors, and automatic screenshots. Returns [@ev:TOKEN] markers to embed in responses. ([ChizhongWang/veriglow-agent-map-skill](https://github.com/ChizhongWang/veriglow-agent-map-skill/tree/HEAD/plugins/citeanything/skills/citeanything) · ★3 · 2 copies)
- **citeanything**: Create replayable CiteAnything evidence citations from inspected web pages or private knowledge-base documents, and build or synchronize durable cited Works such as HTML, PPTX, PDF, Markdown, and source bundles. Use when an agent must substantiate factual claims, emit CiteAnything citation markers or links, turn research into a cited artifact, or move a local Work into the user's CiteAnything account with the first-party CLI. ([veriglow/veriglow-plugins](https://github.com/veriglow/veriglow-plugins/tree/HEAD/plugins/veriglow/skills/citeanything) · ★1)
- **citebench-eval**: Evaluates the capability of citation recommendation models to identify relevant academic references given local citation contexts. It probes robustness across varying contextual features, including context length, reference position, academic field, publication year, citation count, and part-of-speech tags. Use when the user wants to benchmark on S2ORC/S2AG Diagnostic Datasets, or asks about evaluating this task. Reports MRR. ([qhjqhj00/research-skills-pool](https://github.com/qhjqhj00/research-skills-pool/tree/HEAD/skill-factory/output/citebench-eval) · ★3)
- **citecheck**: Use when the user asks to "verify citations", "check references", "validate paper citations", or "evaluate reference relevance". It extracts references from LaTeX/PDF papers, checks formatting rules, verifies existence via Crossref / Semantic Scholar / OpenAlex / PubMed / arXiv / dblp / Google Scholar / WebSearch, and scores thematic/semantic relevance. ([color4-alt/CiteCheck](https://github.com/color4-alt/CiteCheck/tree/HEAD/skills/citecheck) · ★73)
- **citecheck**: Score \cite pertinence in a LaTeX file. Use when the user runs /citecheck on a .tex file or asks to verify references against InspireHEP/arXiv abstracts. ([aurelio-amerio/citecheck](https://github.com/aurelio-amerio/citecheck/tree/HEAD/skills/citecheck) · ★2 · 2 copies)
- **citecheck**: Verify every legal authority cited in a draft (submissions, opinion, memo, article). Extracts citations deterministically, confirms each exists against a source, then checks the authority actually supports the proposition it is cited for. Use before anything with citations leaves the building. ([kevanwee/citecheck](https://github.com/kevanwee/citecheck/blob/HEAD/SKILL.md) · ★1)
- **citecheck-deep**: Deep-check flagged and unresolved citations from a /citecheck run using full arXiv PDFs (Sonnet) and NotebookLM. Use when the user runs /citecheck-deep on a .tex file. ([aurelio-amerio/citecheck](https://github.com/aurelio-amerio/citecheck/tree/HEAD/skills/citecheck-deep) · ★2 · 2 copies)
- **citeck-app-from-spec**: EXPERIMENTAL (stage-1 prototype; the result is checked through Records API, a working UI is not guaranteed). Build a new Citeck ECOS application from a requirements document (ТЗ): plan, write ecos-apps artifact files, check BPMN with the platform, deploy into one workspace, walk the main scenario on the stand, fix and redeploy in a loop, and report requirement by requirement. Use when asked to create or generate an application, business app or process automation from a spec on a Citeck stand. - **citeck-ask-docs**: Ask a question about the Citeck ECOS platform — searches citeck-docs via RAG and synthesizes an answer with citations. Use when the user asks how Citeck works, how to configure something, or about platform concepts. - **citeck-auth**: Configure Citeck ECOS connection - set URL, credentials, and test connectivity. Use when the user needs to set up or manage Citeck authentication. ([Citeck/citeck-claude-code-tools](https://github.com/Citeck/citeck-claude-code-tools/tree/HEAD/plugins/citeck/skills/citeck-auth))
- **citeck-changes-to-task**: Create a Citeck Project Tracker issue from current git changes ([Citeck/citeck-claude-code-tools](https://github.com/Citeck/citeck-claude-code-tools/tree/HEAD/plugins/citeck/skills/citeck-changes-to-task))
- **citeck-changes-to-task-md**: Generate task.md file with structured task description from git changes ([Citeck/citeck-claude-code-tools](https://github.com/Citeck/citeck-claude-code-tools/tree/HEAD/plugins/citeck/skills/citeck-changes-to-task-md))
- **citeck-test-feature**: Design, audit and run smoke, impact or full acceptance/regression testing of Citeck features, branches and tracker issues. ([Citeck/citeck-claude-code-tools](https://github.com/Citeck/citeck-claude-code-tools/tree/HEAD/plugins/citeck/skills/citeck-test-feature))
- **Cited research**: Use when an answer or document has to show where its facts came from, such as "research this and cite your sources", "with citations", "write a report with references", "fact-check this" or "where did you get that?". Numbers each source as it's read, cites while writing, checks quotes. ([ai-2070/athena-agents](https://github.com/ai-2070/athena-agents/tree/HEAD/skills/athena/grounded-citations) · ★2)
- **cited-answer**: Answer a question from the knowledge base with a **grounded, cited** slice — never from memory of the model alone. ([mikeparcewski/wicked-estate](https://github.com/mikeparcewski/wicked-estate/tree/HEAD/crates/wicked-estate-knowledge/skills/cited-answer) · ★2)
